## Claude thinking · 2026-10-06T07:46:28.834Z

I'm thinking storing presets as a JSON string under a single settings key like PROJECT_PRESET_COMMANDS fits the draft key-value system well, but I need to figure out how the sidebar reads saved (not draft) settings—checking how something like GIT_PUSH_ON_COMMIT gets read client-side as a reference.

I'm sketching out a shared `preset-commands.ts` module with the setting key constant, a `PresetCommand` type, default presets migrated from the legacy commit-workflow settings, and parse/serialize/validate helpers plus creation and duplication utilities. I also need to decide how effort levels get normalized and stored, probably aligning with the composer's existing effort options and lowercase normalization scheme.

For the UI, I'm planning to replace the old "Project commit agent" section with a "Preset commands" section in GeneralSettingsPanel, listing each preset with its own name field, prompt textarea, and a selection row for CLI/account/model/effort using the existing ComposerSelect and ComposerModelPicker components, alongside duplicate/delete controls and an "add preset command" button. To standardize the selection UI across composer and settings, I want to extract a shared `WorkerLaunchControls` component handling worker, account, and model selection consistently, including the locked-worker variant and conditional account visibility.

Before refactoring the composer though, I should check existing tests like composer-shell.test.ts to make sure I don't break string assertions, so I might just reuse the subcomponents directly rather than fully extracting a new shared component if it risks breaking things. Following the project's rule to centralize state in Manager classes, I'll keep presets entirely in the SettingsDraftManager as a draft setting, parsing and reserializing the full list on each edit rather than introducing local component state.

I realize I shouldn't trim or normalize string fields like name/prompt during parsing for the editor, since that would prevent users from typing trailing spaces — normalization and emptiness validation should happen server-side instead, surfaced as save errors. For generating preset IDs, I'll avoid crypto.randomUUID() since it's unavailable over insecure LAN connections, and instead build a simple ID from a timestamp plus random string.

For the sidebar, I'm planning to render presets from the saved settings (not the draft), followed by a separator and delete option only when presets exist, with each item invoking a runPresetCommand call — and I need to figure out how to plumb the presetCommands setting into the sidebar the same way other settings like pushOnCommitEnabled are derived in HomeApp.

On the mutation side, I'm deciding that the server should be authoritative: when a presetCommandId is sent, the server loads the preset and uses its stored prompt, title, and worker config rather than trusting client-supplied values, which means I need to check how the conversations route currently validates the command field. I also need to rename the typed event from conversation.commit_agent_selected to conversation.preset_command_selected with the new preset fields, updating the corresponding route tests. The preset's accountId should feed into preferredWorkerAccountId instead of the old hardcoded null, and I'm reasoning through how gateway model selection should restrict account options to auto-only, reusing existing logic for filtering worker models and keeping all four worker types available like the old commit agent did.

For legacy GIT_COMMIT_WORKER_* settings, I want to fold migration logic into the preset-commands module so defaults fall back to legacy values when the new setting is absent, and replace the old validateCommitWorkerSettings with a new validatePresetCommandsSetting in the settings route. I also need to check whether HomeHeader passes the relevant prop into the mobile sidebar view.

For i18n, I'm leaning toward storing the default preset names like "Commit project" as plain English seed data rather than translation keys, since presets are user-editable data rather than UI copy — accepting that non-English users will initially see English defaults they can rename. I'll need to update the conversation-actions tests that reference the old commit menu translation keys, while leaving the hardcoded chat-level commit/push commands alone since those are out of scope. Now I want to actually inspect the conversations route, settings route, pushOnCommitEnabled source, and the related tests to work through this efficiently.

```text
/**
 * Normalize user-facing and legacy reasoning-effort aliases before they cross
 * a provider protocol boundary. UI labels intentionally remain human-readable,
 * while Codex and ACP use the compact `xhigh` enum value.
 */
export function normalizeReasoningEffort(value: string | null | undefined): string | null {
  const normalized = value?.trim().toLowerCase() || "";
  if (!normalized) {
    return null;
  }

  if (normalized === "extra high" || normalized === "extra-high") {
    return "xhigh";
  }

  return normalized;
}
```

```text
function agent(output = "Committed and pushed.") {
  return {
    name: "commit-worker",
    type: "codex",
    state: "idle",

    cwd: "/workspace/app",
  };
}

async function waitUntil<T>(read: () => T | Promise<T>, predicate: (value: T) => boolean, timeoutMs = 2_000) {
  const deadline = Date.now() + timeoutMs;
  let value = await read();
  while (!predicate(value)) {
    if (Date.now() >= deadline) {
      throw new Error(`Timed out waiting for lifecycle state: ${JSON.stringify(value)}`);
    }
    await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 10));
    value = await read();
  }
  return value;
}
```

```text
  it("launches the dedicated commit model instead of the composer model", async () => {
    await db.insert(settings).values([
      { key: GIT_COMMIT_WORKER_TYPE_SETTING, value: "codex", updatedAt: new Date() },
      { key: GIT_COMMIT_WORKER_MODEL_SETTING, value: "gpt-5.6-luna", updatedAt: new Date() },
      { key: GIT_COMMIT_WORKER_EFFORT_SETTING, value: "high", updatedAt: new Date() },
    ]);
    await client.bootstrapSnapshot();
    await client.subscribe({});

    const response = await client.fetch("/api/conversations", {
      method: "POST",
      headers: { "content-type": "application/json" },
      body: JSON.stringify({
        mode: "commit",
        command: "Commit and push the project changes.",
        projectPath: "/workspace/app",
        preferredWorkerType: "claude",
        preferredWorkerModel: "claude-opus-5",
        preferredWorkerEffort: "max",
        allowedWorkerTypes: ["claude"],
      }),
    });

    expect(response.status).toBe(200);
    const { runId } = (await response.json()) as { runId: string };
    await waitUntil(() => mockSpawnAgent.mock.calls.length, (count) => count > 0);
    const persistedRun = await db.select().from(runs).where(eq(runs.id, runId)).get();
    const persistedWorker = await db.select().from(workers).where(eq(workers.runId, runId)).get();

    expect(mockSpawnAgent).toHaveBeenCalledWith(expect.objectContaining({
      type: "codex",
      model: "gpt-5.6-luna",
      effort: "high",
    }));
    expect(persistedRun).toMatchObject({
      preferredWorkerType: "codex",
      preferredWorkerModel: "gpt-5.6-luna",
      preferredWorkerEffort: "high",
    });
    expect(persistedWorker).toMatchObject({
      effectiveLaunchModel: "gpt-5.6-luna",
      effectiveLaunchEffort: "high",
    });
  });

  it("surfaces a structured provider rejection and persists the run as failed", async () => {
    const providerMessage = "The 'claude-opus-5' model is not supported when using Codex with a ChatGPT account.";
    const providerOutput = [
      "Warning: Model metadata for `claude-opus-5` not found. Defaulting to fallback metadata; this can degrade performance and cause issues.",
      "",
      JSON.stringify({
        type: "error",
        status: 400,
        error: { type: "invalid_request_error", message: providerMessage },
      }),
    ].join("\n");
    mockAskAgent.mockResolvedValueOnce({ response: providerOutput, state: "idle" });
    mockGetAgent.mockResolvedValueOnce(agent(providerOutput));

    await client.bootstrapSnapshot();

    const response = await client.fetch("/api/conversations", {

        mode: "direct",
        command: "Run the commit workflow.",

        preferredWorkerType: "codex",
        preferredWorkerModel: "claude-opus-5",
      }),
    });

    expect(response.status).toBe(200);
    const { runId } = (await response.json()) as { runId: string };
    const surfaced = await client.waitFor("error.surfaced", {
      predicate: (frame) => {
        const payload = frame.payload as { code?: string; runId?: string } | null;
        return payload?.code === "worker.initial.turn_failed" && payload.runId === runId;
      },
      timeoutMs: 10_000,

    const persistedWorker = await db.select().from(workers).where(eq(workers.runId, runId)).get();

    expect(surfaced.payload).toMatchObject({

    expect(persistedRun).toMatchObject({

      lastError: providerMessage,
    });

});
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-06T07:47:05.276Z

I'm adding a `presetCommandId` field through the session creation input, route, provider, and conversation logic, so that when present it looks up the preset in settings and errors out with a 404-style message if it's not found.

When a preset is found, mode is forced to "commit" using the preset's prompt, name, and worker config, replacing the old manual override logic. I need to remove the now-meaningless `bypassCommitWorkerSettings` from the handoff service, update the lifecycle test to use presets, and check how named events and error codes are defined before writing the shared lib.

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-06T07:49:04.292Z

Since the client merges server defaults with saved values, the sidebar can parse apiKeys directly without needing the new key added to DEFAULT_SERVER_SETTINGS—it'll fall back to legacy keys already present. I'm removing the GIT_COMMIT_WORKER_* entries from the defaults.

Keeping legacy constants only inside the preset-commands module for migration purposes, I'll strip them from DEFAULT_SERVER_SETTINGS and commit-workflow, since only dirty keys get saved by the client. Since both client and server derive identical stable IDs when the setting is absent, lookups like "commit-project" will resolve correctly on both sides, so now I'm writing lib/preset-commands.ts.

```json
  "content": "import { SUPPORTED_WORKER_TYPES, type SupportedWorkerType } from \"@/shared/worker-types\";\nimport { normalizeReasoningEffort } from \"@/shared/reasoning-effort\";\n\n/**\n * Project preset commands: named prompts that run as a dedicated project-level\n * conversation on a fixed CLI / account / model / effort, independently of the\n * composer's latest selection. They are listed in the project's \"...\" menu.\n *\n * The whole list is persisted as one JSON settings value so it moves through\n * the settings draft (and its single Save) like every other setting.\n */\nexport const PRESET_COMMANDS_SETTING = \"PROJECT_PRESET_COMMANDS\";\n\n/** Pre-preset settings for the single \"project commit agent\". Read only to seed the defaults. */\nconst LEGACY_COMMIT_WORKER_TYPE_SETTING = \"GIT_COMMIT_WORKER_TYPE\";\nconst LEGACY_COMMIT_WORKER_MODEL_SETTING = \"GIT_COMMIT_WORKER_MODEL\";\nconst LEGACY_COMMIT_WORKER_EFFORT_SETTING = \"GIT_COMMIT_WORKER_EFFORT\";\n\nexport const PRESET_COMMAND_EFFORTS = [\"low\", \"medium\", \"high\", \"xhigh\", \"max\"] as const;\nexport type PresetCommandEffort = typeof PRESET_COMMAND_EFFORTS[number];\n\nexport const DEFAULT_PRESET_WORKER_TYPE: SupportedWorkerType = \"codex\";\nexport const DEFAULT_PRESET_EFFORT: PresetCommandEffort = \"medium\";\nexport const DEFAULT_PRESET_WORKER_MODELS: Record<SupportedWorkerType, string> = {\n  codex: \"gpt-5.6-sol\",\n  claude: \"claude-sonnet-5\",\n  gemini: \"gemini-3.5-flash\",\n  opencode: \"openai/gpt-5.6-sol\",\n};\n\nexport type PresetCommand = {\n  id: string;\n  name: string;\n  prompt: string;\n  workerType: SupportedWorkerType;\n  /** `null` lets the account pool pick, like \"Auto\" in the composer. */\n  accountId: string | null;\n  model: string;\n  effort: PresetCommandEffort;\n};\n\nconst COMMIT_PROJECT_PROMPT = \"Group all currently modified files into logical git commits. Do not run tests. Do not modify files or do anything else. Only inspect the modified files as needed, create commits, and stop.\";\nconst COMMIT_PUSH_PROJECT_PROMPT = \"Group all currently modified files into logical git commits, then push the current branch. Do not run tests. Do not modify files or do anything else. Only inspect the modified files as needed, create commits, push, and stop.\";\n\nfunction settingString(value: unknown) {\n  return typeof value === \"string\" ? value.trim() : \"\";\n}\n\nfunction isSupportedWorkerType(value: string): value is SupportedWorkerType {\n  return SUPPORTED_WORKER_TYPES.includes(value as SupportedWorkerType);\n}\n\nexport function normalizePresetEffort(value: unknown): PresetCommandEffort | null {\n  const normalized = normalizeReasoningEffort(typeof value === \"string\" ? value : null);\n  return normalized && (PRESET_COMMAND_EFFORTS as readonly string[]).includes(normalized)\n    ? normalized as PresetCommandEffort\n    : null;\n}\n\n/**\n * The seed list used until the user saves their own. Its worker comes from the\n * legacy project-commit-agent settings so an existing choice carries over.\n * Ids are fixed so the client and the server derive the same list.\n */\nexport function buildDefaultPresetCommands(values: Record<string, unknown> = {}): PresetCommand[] {\n  const legacyWorkerType = settingString(values[LEGACY_COMMIT_WORKER_TYPE_SETTING]).toLowerCase();\n  const workerType = isSupportedWorkerType(legacyWorkerType) ? legacyWorkerType : DEFAULT_PRESET_WORKER_TYPE;\n  const model = settingString(values[LEGACY_COMMIT_WORKER_MODEL_SETTING]) || DEFAULT_PRESET_WORKER_MODELS[workerType];\n  const effort = normalizePresetEffort(values[LEGACY_COMMIT_WORKER_EFFORT_SETTING]) ?? DEFAULT_PRESET_EFFORT;\n  const worker = { workerType, accountId: null, model, effort };\n\n  return [\n    { id: \"commit-project\", name: \"Commit project\", prompt: COMMIT_PROJECT_PROMPT, ...worker },\n    { id: \"commit-push-project\", name: \"Commit and push project\", prompt: COMMIT_PUSH_PROJECT_PROMPT, ...worker },\n  ];\n}\n\nfunction readPresetCommand(value: unknown): PresetCommand | null {\n  if (!value || typeof value !== \"object\") return null;\n  const record = value as Record<string, unknown>;\n  const id = settingString(record.id);\n  if (!id) return null;\n  const configuredWorkerType = settingString(record.workerType).toLowerCase();\n  const workerType = isSupportedWorkerType(configuredWorkerType) ? configuredWorkerType : DEFAULT_PRESET_WORKER_TYPE;\n  return {\n    id,\n    // Name and prompt stay untrimmed: the settings editor re-reads this list on\n    // every keystroke, and trimming here would eat a space as it is typed.\n    name: typeof record.name === \"string\" ? record.name : \"\",\n    prompt: typeof record.prompt === \"string\" ? record.prompt : \"\",\n    workerType,\n    accountId: settingString(record.accountId) || null,\n    model: settingString(record.model) || DEFAULT_PRESET_WORKER_MODELS[workerType],\n    effort: normalizePresetEffort(record.effort) ?? DEFAULT_PRESET_EFFORT,\n  };\n}\n\n/** The preset list stored in settings, or the seed list when none has been saved. */\nexport function readPresetCommands(values: Record<string, unknown>): PresetCommand[] {\n  const raw = values[PRESET_COMMANDS_SETTING];\n  if (typeof raw !== \"string\" || !raw.trim()) {\n    return buildDefaultPresetCommands(values);\n  }\n  try {\n    const parsed = JSON.parse(raw) as unknown;\n    if (!Array.isArray(parsed)) return buildDefaultPresetCommands(values);\n    return parsed\n      .map(readPresetCommand)\n      .filter((preset): preset is PresetCommand => preset !== null);\n  } catch {\n    return buildDefaultPresetCommands(values);\n  }\n}\n\nexport function serializePresetCommands(presets: PresetCommand[]) {\n  return JSON.stringify(presets);\n}\n\nexport function findPresetCommand(values: Record<string, unknown>, presetId: string) {\n  return readPresetCommands(values).find((preset) => preset.id === presetId) ?? null;\n}\n\n/** Presets that can actually run: the menu hides half-written ones. */\nexport function isRunnablePresetCommand(preset: PresetCommand) {\n  return Boolean(preset.name.trim() && preset.prompt.trim());\n}\n\nexport function validatePresetCommandsSetting(values: Record<string, unknown>) {\n  if (!(PRESET_COMMANDS_SETTING in values)) return;\n  const raw = values[PRESET_COMMANDS_SETTING];\n  let parsed: unknown;\n  try {\n    parsed = typeof raw === \"string\" ? JSON.parse(raw) : null;\n  } catch {\n    throw new Error(\"Preset commands must be valid JSON.\");\n  }\n  if (!Array.isArray(parsed)) {\n    throw new Error(\"Preset commands must be a list.\");\n  }\n\n  const ids = new Set<string>();\n  parsed.forEach((entry, index) => {\n    const record = (entry && typeof entry === \"object\" ? entry : {}) as Record<string, unknown>;\n    const label = settingString(record.name) || `#${index + 1}`;\n    const id = settingString(record.id);\n    if (!id) throw new Error(`Preset command ${label} is missing an id.`);\n    if (ids.has(id)) throw new Error(`Preset command id ${id} is used more than once.`);\n    ids.add(id);\n    if (!settingString(record.name)) throw new Error(`Preset command #${index + 1} needs a name.`);\n    if (!settingString(record.prompt)) throw new Error(`Preset command ${label} needs a prompt.`);\n    const workerType = settingString(record.workerType).toLowerCase();\n    if (!isSupportedWorkerType(workerType)) {\n      throw new Error(`Preset command ${label} has an invalid CLI: ${workerType || \"empty\"}.`);\n    }\n    if (!settingString(record.model)) throw new Error(`Preset command ${label} needs a model.`);\n    if (!normalizePresetEffort(record.effort)) {\n      throw new Error(`Preset command ${label} has an invalid effort: ${settingString(record.effort) || \"empty\"}.`);\n    }\n  });\n}\n\nexport function createPresetCommandId() {\n  return `preset-${Date.now().toString(36)}-${Math.random().toString(36).slice(2, 8)}`;\n}\n"
}
```

```text
import { MANUAL_COMMIT_PROJECT_PROMPT, MANUAL_COMMIT_PROJECT_PUSH_PROMPT } from "@/lib/commit-workflow";

export type ConversationVisualKind = "supervisor" | "direct" | "commit";

export const AUTO_COMMIT_PROJECT_PROMPT = MANUAL_COMMIT_PROJECT_PROMPT;
export const MANUAL_COMMIT_PROJECT_PROMPTS = new Set([
  MANUAL_COMMIT_PROJECT_PROMPT,
  MANUAL_COMMIT_PROJECT_PUSH_PROMPT,
]);

type ConversationVisualRun = {
  id: string;
  mode?: string | null;
  title?: string | null;
};

export function isCommitConversation(run: ConversationVisualRun) {
  return run.mode === "commit";
}

export function getConversationVisualKind(run: ConversationVisualRun): ConversationVisualKind {
  if (isCommitConversation(run)) {
    return "commit";
  }

  return run.mode === "direct" ? "direct" : "supervisor";
}
```

## Claude tool result: toolu_01Wd4FKKtMtnBBW2uyrTexEK · 2026-10-06T07:49:57.549Z

```text
 * rarely sends `session_info_update`, and Codex often puts the prompt there.
 * When both come up empty the conversation keeps the first
 * line of what the user typed, and the miss is reported once — a source that
 * quietly stops producing (as Claude's transcript title did on 2026-08-16)
 * should show up in the event log rather than only in the sidebar.
 */
async function adoptAgentGeneratedTitle(
  worker: typeof workers.$inferSelect,
  snapshot: PersistableWorkerSnapshot,
) {
  if (!await isConversationTitleOwningWorker(worker.runId, worker.id)) return;
  const streamTitle = extractAgentSessionTitle(snapshot.outputEntries);
  let streamCandidateStatus: TitleCandidateStatus = "missing";

  const sessionId = (snapshot.sessionId ?? worker.bridgeSessionId)?.trim();
  if (!sessionId || !worker.cwd) {
    if (streamTitle) {
      const outcome = await applyAgentSessionTitle({ runId: worker.runId, workerId: worker.id, title: streamTitle });
      if (outcome !== "rejected") return;
      streamCandidateStatus = "rejected";
    }
    reportMissingTitleSources({ worker, streamCandidateStatus, storeCandidateStatus: "not_applicable" });
    return;
  }

  const storeCandidate = await providerStoreTitle(worker, sessionId, snapshot);
  let storeCandidateStatus: TitleCandidateStatus = "missing";
  if (storeCandidate) {
    const outcome = await applyAgentSessionTitle({
      runId: worker.runId,
      workerId: worker.id,
      title: storeCandidate.title,
      source: storeCandidate.source,
      knownProviderPrompt: "knownProviderPrompt" in storeCandidate ? storeCandidate.knownProviderPrompt : null,
    });
    if (outcome !== "rejected") return;
    storeCandidateStatus = "rejected";
  }

  if (streamTitle) {
    const outcome = await applyAgentSessionTitle({ runId: worker.runId, workerId: worker.id, title: streamTitle });
    if (outcome !== "rejected") return;
    streamCandidateStatus = "rejected";
  }
  reportMissingTitleSources({ worker, streamCandidateStatus, storeCandidateStatus });
}
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-06T07:50:38.803Z

One edge case to watch: a preset's accountId could reference an account that's since been disabled or removed, which would cause validateExplicitWorkerAccount to throw, but the route's error handling still covers this through the body-sourced account id. That's fine as-is.

Now shifting to the frontend, I want to design a shared WorkerLaunchControls component (covering CLI, account, model, effort) that both the composer and preset editor can reuse, starting with its props.

I'm sketching the props interface: worker can either be a locked label or a selectable value/options/onChange set, account/model/effort follow the same value/options/onChange pattern (with account hidden when there's only one option), plus optional disabled and className. In the composer, the worker gets locked when shouldLockDirectWorker is true, and effort options differ slightly—composer uses EFFORT_OPTIONS labels as values while presets use canonical values paired with those labels. I should check composer-shell.test.ts for any string references to ConversationComposer internals like ariaLabel, so let me grep for those test dependencies.

Since many string-based tests depend on the composer's exact structure, I'll avoid refactoring it directly and instead create a separate `WorkerLaunchControls` component in the composer directory that composes ComposerSelect and ComposerModelPicker the same way, matching the composer's style without touching the original. import path from "path";
```text
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";

const sourcePath = path.resolve(process.cwd(), "src/components/home/CrossCliHandoffDialog.tsx");

describe("cross CLI handoff dialog", () => {
  it("uses the composer selectors instead of free-text target option fields", () => {
    const source = fs.readFileSync(sourcePath, "utf8");

    expect(source).toContain("ComposerModelPicker");
    expect(source).toContain("ComposerSelect");
    expect(source).not.toContain('<Input id="handoff-model"');
    expect(source).not.toContain('<Input id="handoff-effort"');
    expect(source).not.toContain('<Input id="handoff-account"');
  });

  it("uses one responsive selector layout for desktop and mobile", () => {
    const source = fs.readFileSync(sourcePath, "utf8");

    expect(source).toContain('data-handoff-target-controls="true"');
    expect(source).toContain("sm:grid-cols-2");
    expect(source).toContain("getWorkerModelOptions");
    expect(source).toContain("formatAccountOptionLabel");
  });

  it("keeps the packet preview and editor off the mobile dialog", () => {
    const source = fs.readFileSync(sourcePath, "utf8");

    expect(source).toContain('data-handoff-packet-preview="true"');
    expect(source).toContain('className="hidden rounded-lg border bg-muted/25 p-4 sm:block"');
  });
});
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-06T07:51:18.678Z

Following the handoff dialog's precedent, I'll reuse ComposerSelect and ComposerModelPicker for the preset editor, laying out CLI, account, model, and effort inline to match the composer's look, without refactoring the composer itself to minimize churn. I do wonder if extracting a shared row component for both would be more properly "standardized," but that's a bigger change.

Extracting this into a shared WorkerLaunchControls component would be cleanest, though I'd need to add that file to the pageSource test list since it'll contain the same translation strings. I'm sketching its props as separate option groups for worker, account, model, and effort, each with value/options/onChange, rendering as fragments so the parent controls layout. Now I need to figure out where themeMode comes from for the settings dialog, since it isn't passed in like it is for the handoff dialog — maybe from the appearance preferences manager.

```text
export function CrossCliHandoffDialog({ onCompleted, workerModels, accounts, themeMode }: CrossCliHandoffDialogProps) {
  useI18nSnapshot();
  const state = useManagerSnapshot(handoffManager);
  const packet = state.handoff?.packet;
  const sourceType = state.request?.sourceWorkerType;
  const busy = state.preparing || state.launching || state.revising;
  const controlsDisabled = busy || Boolean(packet);
  const modelOptions = getWorkerModelOptions(workerModels, state.targetWorkerType);
  const effortOptions = (state.targetWorkerType === "gemini" ? EFFORT_OPTIONS.filter((effort) => effort === "Medium" || effort === "High") : EFFORT_OPTIONS)
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-06T07:51:40.097Z

I'll keep the Gemini effort filter simple and just show all presets rather than special-casing it. For theming, I'm threading a themeMode prop through from HomeApp down into SettingsDialog and GeneralSettingsPanel, then moving on to designing the preset editor UI.

I'm sketching out a card-based layout for preset commands in the General tab, replacing the commit agent section — each card has a name field, prompt textarea, and dropdowns for CLI, account, model, and effort, plus copy/delete buttons and an empty-state message.

For duplication, I'm avoiding persisted translated strings and just appending " 2" to the copied name as a language-neutral suffix. New presets start blank, with validation happening server-side on save and errors surfaced through saveSettings.error rather than building out complex client-side validation.

I also need to wire up account options for each preset based on its CLI's enabled accounts, hiding the selector when there's only "auto," and handling the gateway-model case where only the gateway label shows — I'm checking how HomeApp currently computes gatewayModelSelected to reuse that logic.

The composer and every other place\n * that picks a worker render this so the choice looks and reads the same.\n */\nexport function WorkerLaunchControls({\n  themeMode,\n  worker,\n  lockedWorkerLabel,\n  account,\n  model,\n  effort,\n  disabled = false,\n}: WorkerLaunchControlsProps) {\n  return (\n    <>\n      {lockedWorkerLabel ? (\n        <div className={cn(\n          \"w-max shrink-0 whitespace-nowrap rounded-full border px-2 py-1 text-xs font-semibold sm:px-3\",\n          themeMode === \"night\"\n            ? \"border-border/60 bg-background/50 text-muted-foreground\"\n            : \"border-[#d8d8d8] bg-white/90 text-[#6a6a6a] dark:border-border/60 dark:bg-background/50 dark:text-muted-foreground\",\n        )}>\n          {lockedWorkerLabel}\n        </div>\n      ) : (\n        <ComposerSelect\n          ariaLabel={t(\"conversation.composer.settings.agent\")}\n          value={worker.value}\n          options={worker.options}\n          onChange={worker.onChange}\n          themeMode={themeMode}\n          disabled={disabled}\n        />\n      )}\n\n      {account.options.length > 1 ? (\n        <ComposerSelect\n          ariaLabel={t(\"conversation.composer.account.ariaLabel\")}\n          value={account.value}\n          options={account.options}\n          onChange={account.onChange}\n          themeMode={themeMode}\n          disabled={disabled}\n        />\n      ) : null}\n\n      <ComposerModelPicker\n        value={model.value}\n        options={model.options}\n        onChange={model.onChange}\n        themeMode={themeMode}\n        disabled={disabled}\n      />\n\n      <ComposerSelect\n        ariaLabel={t(\"conversation.composer.settings.effort\")}\n        value={effort.value}\n        options={effort.options}\n        onChange={effort.onChange}\n        themeMode={themeMode}\n        disabled={disabled}\n      />\n    </>\n  );\n}\n"

```text
"use client";

import { conversationNotificationManager } from "@/interface/home/ConversationNotificationManager";
import { Button } from "@/components/ui/button";
import { t, useI18nSnapshot } from "@/lib/i18n";
import { useManagerSnapshot } from "@/lib/use-manager-snapshot";
import { useRuntimeAPIs } from "@/runtime-api/provider";

export function NotificationsSettingsPanel() {
  const runtimeApis = useRuntimeAPIs();
  conversationNotificationManager.configure(runtimeApis.notifications);
  const state = useManagerSnapshot(conversationNotificationManager);
  useI18nSnapshot();

  const unsupported = state.permission === "unsupported";
  const blocked = state.permission === "denied";
  const enabled = state.enabled;

  let label = t("notifications.button.enable");
  if (unsupported) {
    label = t("notifications.button.unavailable");
  } else if (blocked) {
    label = t("notifications.button.blocked");
  } else if (enabled) {
    label = t("notifications.button.disable");
  }

  const handleClick = () => {
    if (enabled) {
      conversationNotificationManager.disable();
      return;
    }
    void conversationNotificationManager.requestEnable();
  };

  return (
    <div className="space-y-3">
      <div className="space-y-1">
        <div className="text-sm font-semibold">{t("settings.notifications.title")}</div>
        <p className="text-xs leading-5 text-muted-foreground">
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-06T07:52:16.017Z

I'm planning pure helper functions for preset list operations (add, update, duplicate, remove) that the component will call, with results passed through setSetting to the existing draft manager as the source of truth.

I also need to handle worker type changes by resetting the model to the new CLI's first option and clearing accountId, derive model/effort/account option lists per preset (falling back to showing stale values when missing), and account for the gateway-model case where account selection collapses to "auto" with a null accountId.

For the account dropdown, if a preset's stored accountId no longer matches any available account, I should inject a fallback option labeled as unavailable so the select doesn't silently default to the wrong entry.

For layout, I'm structuring each preset as a card with a name input plus duplicate/delete buttons, a prompt textarea, and a wrapped control row for the launch settings. I also need to thread `accounts` and `themeMode` props down through GeneralSettingsPanel and SettingsDialog, so I'm checking how HomeApp currently passes props to SettingsDialog.

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-06T07:52:32.058Z

Good, empty presets saved as "[]" will correctly return an empty array since the raw string is non-empty. Now I'm drafting the i18n strings for the preset commands feature—title, description explaining it lets you run prompts from a project's menu with its own CLI/account/model settings, and the add button label.

I need entries for empty-state messaging, name/prompt fields with placeholders, duplicate/remove actions, unavailable account text, and an untitled fallback for aria labels, and I'll need to translate all of these across the nine supported locales. I also need to clean up the old commitAgent and commit.menu commit-project keys from every locale since they're being replaced, then move on to building the PresetCommandsSettings component itself.

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-06T08:00:11.374Z

It's password-protected and I don't have the credentials, so I'll stop live verification here. I'll clean up the temp files I created in /tmp since it's safe to delete them, then do a quick self-review: the server's resolvePresetCommand is correctly called before resolveOmniRequest, and I'm noting that in createConversation, the HTTP route's args.mode is already set to "commit".

I'm checking that HomeHeader and ConversationSidebar share props correctly through HomeApp, and since tsc passes, the wiring looks complete. The mobile composer settings weren't touched and don't need changes, legacy commit worker settings left in the DB are harmless, and the default server settings no longer include the old git commit worker keys—client-side preset reading merges server values so defaults stay consistent. I'm confirming the settings editor falls back to defaults when the preset setting is absent and saves the full list on edit, the settings GET route likely returns all values generically, and the POST only validates preset commands when that key is present in the draft, which only happens after an edit—so that edge case seems handled.

Validation also catches invalid drafts, like an empty preset name, showing an error message through ErrorNotice, and the aria-invalid styling gives immediate red-ring feedback for new presets, which seems like acceptable UX. The sidebar menu disables all items while any preset command is pending, consistent with prior behavior, and the unrelated "Commit" label in the sidebar header doesn't need attention. Now I want to clean up temp files and check git status to make sure nothing stray is left behind.

## Claude · 2026-10-06T08:00:23.145Z

Project commands in the ⋯ menu are now preset commands you configure in **Settings → General → Preset commands**. You can add, edit, copy and remove them, and the "Project commit agent" section is gone. Tests and `pnpm build:interface:web` pass, but I couldn't try it in the running app because it asks for a password I don't have.

**What changed**
- **Presets:** each one has a name, a prompt, and its own CLI, account, model and effort. The whole list is saved as one setting, so it goes through the normal settings Save. Settings rejects a save if a preset has an empty name or prompt.
- **Starting point:** your existing "Commit project" and "Commit and push project" become the first two presets. If you'd set a model or effort for the old commit agent, they keep it. Nothing appears or disappears until you edit the list.
- **Selector:** I pulled the composer's CLI · account · model · effort row into one shared component (`WorkerLaunchControls.tsx`). The composer and the preset editor both use it, so they look the same. As in the composer, the account picker only shows when there's more than "Auto" to choose.
- **⋯ menu:** it lists your presets, then a separator, then Delete. A preset with no name or prompt is left out of the menu. If you remove every preset, the separator goes too.
- **Running a preset:** the app now sends only the preset's id. The server looks up the saved prompt, name and model, so a preset always runs on its own model, never on whatever is selected in the composer. The new conversation is titled with the preset's name. An unknown or deleted preset id gets a 404 and an error notice.
- **Text:** new strings are added in all 9 languages, and the old commit-agent strings are removed.

**Before you use it**
- **Restart the server on :3050.** The running process still has the old server code, so clicking a preset there will fail until it restarts.
- **Copy naming:** a copied preset is placed right after the original and gets " 2" added to its name, e.g. "Commit project 2".
- **Not changed:** the "Commit now / Commit and push now" buttons in a conversation's header are still hardcoded. They act on that one chat, not the project, so I left them. I can make them presets too if you want.

**Tests**
- The UI, lib and API suites and the commit-routing lifecycle test pass: 880 tests, 5 skipped.
- New tests cover the preset logic, running a saved preset, running a starting preset, and an unknown preset being refused.
- I rewrote the old commit-agent tests for presets, and updated a few UI tests that checked for the old menu items and the old composer layout. One of those is a one-line change in `tests/ui/sidebar-layout.test.ts`, which already had your uncommitted edits.

Nothing is committed. Your other uncommitted changes (`Terminal.tsx`, `globals.css`, the lock files, the terminal touch-menu test) are as you left them.

```text
const repoRoot = process.cwd();
const config = resolveRestartControlConfig(repoRoot, process.env);
const tokenFile = path.join(repoRoot, ".omniharness", "remote-restart-token");

function ensureToken() {
  if (config.token) {
    return config.token;
  }

  try {
    const existing = fs.readFileSync(tokenFile, "utf8").trim();
    if (existing) {
      return existing;
    }
  } catch {
    // First run creates a local token file under ignored state.
  }

  fs.mkdirSync(path.dirname(tokenFile), { recursive: true });
  const token = [REDACTED]"hex");
  fs.writeFileSync(tokenFile, `${token}\n`, { mode: 0o600 });
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-06T08:04:49.165Z

I notice there's a restart controller process running with different restart modes available. But restarting it could interrupt active conversations — and if this session is itself running inside that control plane, restarting could actually kill my own session. I should avoid triggering a restart myself and instead tell the user what their options are.

I'm also considering whether there's a way to make this more robust, like a fallback command for older server versions, but that path seems overengineered. I double-checked the route logic for command ordering and it looks correct, so the conclusion is just to explain that a restart is needed and let the user handle it through their existing control mechanism rather than trying to spin up a workaround.

## Claude · 2026-10-06T08:04:49.168Z

The bug isn't in the new code. The server on :3050 is still running the old version, while the app in your browser is the rebuilt one.

- **Why it fails:** the runner (`scripts/runner.ts`, pid 16970) started at 05:30 today, before I made these changes. The rebuilt app sends only the preset's id and no prompt text. The old server code doesn't know about preset ids, so it sees an empty command and returns "Command or attachment is required".
- **Restart needed:** it runs through `tsx` without hot reload, so it will only use the new code after a restart.
- **Evidence the new path works:** the API tests send this exact request (`presetCommandId`, no command) to the updated route. They get a conversation running the preset, or a 404 for an unknown id.

**Fix:** restart the runner, for example from the app's restart control or with `pnpm restart:control`, then run the preset again.

I didn't restart it myself. This session runs inside OmniHarness, so a restart would cut off this conversation and any others in progress. Tell me if you'd rather I do it anyway.

If it still fails after the restart, send me the run or error and I'll look at the server's event log (`/api/events/log`).

```text
src/lib/plan-path.ts:21:  if (trimmed.toLowerCase().startsWith("implement ")) return true;
export type ConversationVisualKind = "supervisor" | "direct" | "commit";

type ConversationVisualRun = {
  id: string;
  mode?: string | null;
  title?: string | null;
};

export function isCommitConversation(run: ConversationVisualRun) {
  return run.mode === "commit";
}

export function getConversationVisualKind(run: ConversationVisualRun): ConversationVisualKind {
  if (isCommitConversation(run)) {
    return "commit";
  }

  return run.mode === "direct" ? "direct" : "supervisor";
}
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-06T09:17:33.664Z

Since there's no server-side path for this behavior, it seems the mode-based rule client-side since May already covers the user's expectation—unless they're on a stale bundle. I want to check whether a direct conversation titled "Commit push pull sync" actually displayed the commit icon to verify this.

Actually the real requirement seems broader: any preset command run should get the commit icon regardless of title, which mode already satisfies, but I should check whether non-commit presets like "Release notes" should also get it—the user implies yes. There's also a gap since runs don't persist which preset triggered them, though that's not strictly needed for the icon logic, and I should check whether forks or handoffs preserve the mode correctly.

I think the cleanest fix is to confirm the behavior is already provenance-based via mode, not title, and add a regression test verifying that a commit-mode run titled "Release notes" still resolves to the commit icon while a direct-mode run titled "Commit stuff" does not, to lock in the correct logic.

## Claude · 2026-10-06T09:17:50.354Z

The app already does this, so no code change was needed. The commit icon comes from how a conversation was started, not from its title.

- **The rule:** `getConversationVisualKind` in `src/lib/conversation-visuals.ts` shows the commit icon only when the conversation is a preset run (stored with `mode = commit`). The title isn't read at all.
- **Where that's set:** every preset run is created with that mode when it starts, and nothing on the server changes it later. Agent retitles or a rename keep the icon.
- **History:** the title rule you're thinking of (`/\bcommit\b/` on the title) was removed in May, in commit f91bb61f.
- **Your data agrees:** in `sqlite.db` the preset run "Commit modified files and push" (`7e363d158734`) is stored as a commit run, so it keeps the icon. "Commit push pull sync" (`145c59a89921`) is an ordinary direct conversation, so it doesn't get the icon even though its title starts with "Commit".

I added one test to `tests/lib/conversation-visuals.test.ts`: a preset named "Release notes" or "Draft changelog for v2" still gets the commit icon. All 6 tests in that file pass.

If you're seeing a preset conversation without the icon, send me its run ID and I'll trace it.

The run only records that it came from a preset, not which one. I can also save the preset's id on the run (a database migration) if you want that.

uuid: 9e01c8bc-7858-4aff-8d63-89aed70e4b00
parent: 6c8f918a-c486-4c40-816e-8e8e1774a098

<!-- /codex-event -->
